# Read, Recall, Colour: full audit, critique and fix plan

Date: 2026-09-09. Method: `$impeccable audit` then `$impeccable critique` over the whole drill: the door (library and section pages), the briefing page, the runner, the handover and marking wait, and the Report Card. Four surfaces, one journey. Evidence: source read in full (about 9,000 lines), the dev server at localhost driven in the in-app browser (lab at `/activity-lab`, harnesses at `/src/dev/section-launch-preview.html` and `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html`), contrast computed from rendered colours through a canvas, and the impeccable 4.1.0 detector on files and rendered URLs. Related: [[RRC-BRIEFING-CRITIQUE-2026-09-08]], [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]], [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]].

What was not possible: the browser pane returned black frames for scrolled or emulated screenshots, so below-the-fold and phone checks are DOM-measured rather than eyeballed. The briefing page has no harness and was audited from code. The overlay mode of the detector does not exist in 4.1.0 (`impeccable live` is a variant tool needing a config file), so the rendered-URL scan replaced it. The first-run walkthrough card and the phone "Next stimulus" button are not rendered by the lab.

## Part 1: Audit (technical)

### Scores by surface

| Surface | A11y | Perf | Responsive | Theming | Anti-patterns | Total /20 | Band |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Door and briefing | 2 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 11 | Acceptable |
| Runner (stage, ready beat, pause, leave, finish) | 2 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 4 | 14 | Good |
| Marking wait and Report Card | 2 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 14 | Good |
| **Journey** | **2** | **3** | **3** | **2** | **3** | **13** | **Acceptable** |

Issue counts across the journey: P0 3, P1 16, P2 24, P3 15. The three P0s are copy and flow, not pixels, and they are in Part 2.

### Anti-patterns verdict, per surface

- **Door:** not first-order slop (nobody guesses "LSAT drill, lavender dusk over a meadow"), but caught by the second-order reflex: the editorial-serif, mono-label, pastel-sky, floating-demo-card lane is the saturated one.      1	import { useMemo } from "react";

     3	
     4	/**
     5	 * The dusk meadow behind the Read, Recall, Colour door.
     6	 *
     7	 * Painted, not photographed, and painted at render time: every ridge is a
     8	 * seeded sum of sines, every blade of grass a seeded quadratic, so the scene
     9	 * ships as geometry rather than as three PNGs that have to arrive. It is one
    10	 * picture cut into five depth planes -- sky, far hills, mid hills, near hills,
    11	 * foreground grass -- and each plane is its own layer so the door can move them
    12	 * at different rates as she scrolls. The demo card sits BETWEEN the near hills
    13	 * and the grass, which is what makes the grass pass in front of it.
    14	 *
    15	 * Palette is the runner's own indigo field lifted into a sky, so the door and
    16	 * the drill room read as one evening.
    17	 */
    18	
    19	const W = 1600;
    20	const H = 900;
    21	
    22	function mulberry32(seed: number) {
    23	  let t = seed;
    24	  return () => {
    25	    t += 0x6d2b79f5;
    26	    let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);
    27	    r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);
    28	    return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
    29	  };
    30	}
    31	
    32	/** A smooth ridge: a few sines summed, closed to the bottom edge. */
    33	function ridge(seed: number, base: number, amp: number): string {
    34	  const rng = mulberry32(seed);
    35	  const waves = Array.from({ length: 4 }, (_, i) => ({
    36	    f: (0.6 + rng() * 1.4) * (i + 1) * 0.0018,
    37	    p: rng() * Math.PI * 2,
    38	    a: amp / (i + 1.2),
    39	  }));
    40	  const pts: string[] = [];
    41	  for (let x = -40; x <= W + 40; x += 16) {
    42	    const y = waves.reduce((s, w) => s + w.a * Math.sin(w.f * x + w.p), base);
    43	    pts.push(`${x.toFixed(1)} ${y.toFixed(1)}`);
    44	  }
    45	  // Closed 700 units below the frame, not at its edge: the plane rises as she
    46	  // scrolls, and the skirt is what keeps the ground beneath it painted.
    47	  return `M -40 ${H + 700} L ${pts.join(" L ")} L ${W + 40} ${H + 700} Z`;
    48	}
    49

    51	  d: string;
    52	  w: number;
    53	  c: string;
    54	  delay: number;

    56	}

    62	}

    65	const FLOWERS = ["#f2b544", "#f7d27a", "#f6f3ea", "#c9c3ec", "#f3c9a0"];
    66	
    67	function grass(seed: number, count: number): { blades: Blade[]; flowers: Flower[] } {

    86	        y: y0 - h - 3,

    88	        c: FLOWERS[Math.floor(rng() * FLOWERS.length)],
    89	      });
    90	    }
    91	  }
    92	  return { blades, flowers };

export function SectionLibraryLaunch({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  children,
  onExit,
}: SectionLibraryLaunchProps) {
  return (
    <SectionLaunchFrame
      title={title}
      subtitle={subtitle}
      instructions={instructions}
      onExit={onExit}
      showBreadcrumbs={false}
      sectionsFirst
    >
      <div className="sl-runs">
        <div>
          <span className="sl-label">Your sections</span>
          <div style={{ marginTop: 12 }}>{children}</div>
        </div>

        <aside className="sl-aside">
          <span className="sl-label">Before you start</span>
          <MethodSummary />
          <p>
            Your entire section is recorded in <strong>one take</strong>. You
            can pause: the text hides and the clock stops while you are away,
            and you cannot go back to a stimulus.
          </p>
          <p>
            Every completed attempt stays here. If you run a section again, it
            is saved as a new attempt. Your earlier attempts are never replaced.
          </p>
        </aside>
      </div>
    </SectionLaunchFrame>
  );
}
=== SectionLaunch 110,165 ===
    [history],
  );
  const stats = useMemo(
    () => statsFor(runs, timeGoalMinutes ?? null),
    [runs, timeGoalMinutes],
  );

  return (
    <SectionLaunchFrame
      title={title}
      subtitle={subtitle}
      instructions={instructions}
      onExit={onExit}
      meta={
        <>
          {sourceSection ? `${sourceSection} · ` : ""}
          {typeof itemCount === "number" ? `${itemCount} stimuli` : ""}
          {timeGoalMinutes ? ` · est ${timeGoalMinutes} min` : ""}
        </>
      }
      primaryAction={
        <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
          Start the section
          <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
        </button>
      }
      closingAction={
        <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
          Start now
          <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
        </button>
      }
      /* Warned, not refused: Joshua's call, 2026-09-04. She is told what the
         run needs and left free to make the call herself, which is why nothing
         here disables Start. The run itself carries an on-screen way to advance
         so that going ahead is a real choice rather than a trap. */
      notice={
        noKeyboard ? (
          <p className="sl-notice" role="status">
            This one is built for a laptop. It records you for the whole section
            in one take, so a call or a locked screen ends the run, and the
            microphone on a phone hears you less well. You can carry on if you
            want to.
          </p>
        ) : null
      }
      strip={showHistory ? <StatStrip state={history} stats={stats} /> : null}
    >
      {showHistory && <RunHistory state={history} />}
    </SectionLaunchFrame>
  );
}

=== 1,140 ===
/* The Read, Recall, Colour door.
 *
 * One evening, in two registers. The hero is the runner's own indigo lifted
 * into a dusk sky over a painted meadow, cut into depth planes that move at
 * their own rates as she scrolls; everything under it sits on cream paper in
 * the same light. The method is performed before it is explained, the four
 * steps are the next thing she meets, and the sections she can run sit
 * directly beneath.
 *
 * Every value is scoped to `.sl`. Nothing here touches the runner
 * (`recall/section.css`) or the shell outside this door. Class names beginning
 * `sl-` are the door's contract with its tests and its two wrappers; `rd-`
 * names are the design and may change freely.
 */

.sl {
  /* The hero panel's ink. Named so a rule that borrows it onto cream is
     visible as a mistake instead of reading as an ordinary colour. */
  --rd-on-dark: #fff;
  --rd-cream: #f6f3ea;
  --rd-paper: #fffdf8;
  --rd-paper-2: #f3efe4;
  --rd-ink: #16132b;
  --rd-ink-2: #46435f;
  --rd-ink-3: #6a6784;
  --rd-line: #e3ded2;
  --rd-line-2: #ede9df;
  --rd-indigo: #1a1635;
  --rd-indigo-2: #2a2450;
  --rd-lavender: #c9c3ec;
  --rd-apricot: #f3c9a0;
  --rd-apricot-light: #ffe8c8;
  --rd-marigold: #f2b544;
  --rd-marigold-ink: #6e4600;
  --rd-marigold-wash: rgba(242, 181, 68, 0.26);
  --rd-emerald: #0e6f4d;
  --rd-emerald-wash: rgba(14, 111, 77, 0.11);
  --rd-display: "Fraunces", "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;
  --rd-sans: "Inter", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif;
  --rd-mono: "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace;
  --rd-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
  --rd-radius: 22px;
  --rd-radius-panel: 28px;

  min-height: 100%;
  background: var(--rd-cream);
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-family: var(--rd-sans);
  font-size: 16px;
  line-height: 1.5;
  -webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased;
  accent-color: var(--rd-ink);
  caret-color: var(--rd-marigold-ink);
  scrollbar-color: #c9c3b6 var(--rd-cream);
}
.sl ::selection {
  background: var(--rd-marigold-wash);
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
.sl :focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink);
  outline-offset: 3px;
  box-shadow: none;
  border-radius: 6px;
}
.sl em {
  font-style: italic;
}

/* ---- the one micro-label style ---- */
.sl-label {
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 11px;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}

/* =============================================================== hero */

.rd-hero {
  padding: 14px 14px 0;
}
.rd-hero-panel {
  position: relative;
  isolation: isolate;
  overflow: hidden;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  min-height: calc(100svh - 28px);
  border-radius: var(--rd-radius-panel);
  background: #2a2450;
  color: var(--rd-on-dark);
}

/* The scene: five planes and a stage, each its own layer. */
.mw {
  position: absolute;
  inset: 0;
  z-index: 0;
  overflow: hidden;
}
.mw-layer {
  position: absolute;
  inset: -6% 0 0 0;
  width: 100%;
  height: 106%;
  display: block;
  overflow: visible;
  will-change: transform;
}
.mw-sky {
  z-index: 1;
}
.mw-far {
  z-index: 2;
}
.mw-mid {
  z-index: 3;
}
.mw-near {
  z-index: 4;
}
.mw-grain {
  z-index: 5;
  inset: 0;
  height: 100%;
  opacity: 0.13;
  mix-blend-mode: soft-light;
  pointer-events: none;
}
.mw-stage {
  position: absolute;
  left: 0;
  right: 0;
  bottom: 11%;
  z-index: 6;
  display: flex;
  justify-content: center;
  padding: 0 22px;

     1	import fs from "node:fs";
     2	import path from "node:path";
     3	import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
     4	
     5	const CSS = fs.readFileSync(path.join(__dirname, "section-launch.css"), "utf8");
     6	
     7	/**
     8	 * A `var(--name)` whose custom property is never defined does not fail loudly.
     9	 * It falls back, or it inherits, and the result is text nobody can read that no
    10	 * test notices. `--rd-ink-soft` did exactly that here: two rules asked for it,
    11	 * it had never been declared, and the section headings quietly rendered fainter
    12	 * than the body copy underneath them.
    13	 *
    14	 * CLAUDE.md carries the same warning from an earlier round, where three token
    15	 * names shipped undefined for five months and removed the focus ring from every
    16	 * shared Button.
    17	 */
    18	describe("section-launch.css", () => {
    19	  const defined = new Set([...CSS.matchAll(/(--rd-[a-z0-9-]+)\s*:/g)].map((m) => m[1]));
    20	  const used = [...CSS.matchAll(/var\((--rd-[a-z0-9-]+)/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
    21	
    22	  it("defines every --rd- token it uses", () => {
    23	    const missing = [...new Set(used)].filter((n) => !defined.has(n));
    24	    expect(missing).toEqual([]);
    25	  });
    26	
    27	  it("gives the two buttons their own focus ring, not the world's", () => {
    28	    // `.sl :focus-visible` sets one ring for the whole world and loses the
    29	    // cascade on these two classes, so Start and Back matched :focus-visible
    30	    // and computed outline-style: none. Measured in the browser, not inferred.
    31	    expect(CSS).toContain(".sl .sl-start:focus-visible");
    32	    expect(CSS).toContain(".sl .sl-brief:focus-visible");
    33	    // The door draws both on a dark panel, where an ink ring is invisible.
    34	    expect(CSS).toContain(".rd-hero .sl-start:focus-visible");
    35	  });
    36	
    37	  it("draws no text in flat white on the briefing page, which sits on cream", () => {
    38	    // The hero classes are white for the door's dark panel. Any of them reused
    39	    // on .sl-briefing-page must restate a colour.
    40	    const scoped = [...CSS.matchAll(/\.sl-briefing-page\s+\.([a-z0-9-]+)\s*\{([^}]*)\}/g)];
    41	    for (const [, cls, body] of scoped) {
    42	      expect(`${cls}: ${body}`).not.toMatch(new RegExp(String.raw`color:\s*(#fff\b|var\(--rd-on-dark\))`, "i"));
    43	    }
    44	  });
    45	});

     1	/**
     2	 * A preview harness for the section launch surface. Development only.
     3	 *
     4	 * It exists so the component can be looked at in a real browser, in every state
     5	 * and at every breakpoint, without signing in and without a graded section
     6	 * sitting in the database. Only the network is stubbed: `window.fetch` is
     7	 * replaced before anything imports the Supabase client, so the component, the
     8	 * client helper and the derivation all run exactly as they do in the product.
     9	 *
    10	 * Not routed and not imported by the app. Reached at
    11	 * `/src/dev/section-launch-preview.html` on the dev server. It is not an entry
    12	 * point in the Vite build, which only builds `index.html`, so it never ships.
    13	 *
    14	 * States: ?state=runs|oneRun|empty|failed|noHistory|library  ·  ?theme=light|dark
    15	 */
    16	import { createRoot } from "react-dom/client";
    17	// The section JSONs deliberately ship an empty instructions block -- the rules
    18	// are authored once, in one file, and copied in at seed time -- so the harness
    19	// reads the same instructions file the migration seeds.
    20	import sectionInstructions from "../features/activity-centre/content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";
    21	import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../features/activity-centre/api/types";
    22	
    23	const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);
    24	const state = params.get("state") ?? "runs";
    25	const theme = params.get("theme") ?? "light";
    26	
    27	function stim(item_order: number, over: Record<string, unknown> = {}) {
    28	  return {
    29	    slug: `s${item_order}`,
    30	    item_order,
    31	    carried: 3,
    32	    total: 3,
    33	    percent: 100,
    34	    band: "at-the-standard",
    35	    missed: [],
    36	    decoysCarried: [],
    37	    unjudged: [],
    38	    reciting: false,
    39	    listing: false,
    40	    coloured: true,
    41	    colourSpecific: true,
    42	    dead_air_seconds: 0,
    43	    spoken: "she said something here",
    44	    ...over,
    45	  };
    46	}
    47	
    48	const SLOTS = 25;
    49	function run(seconds: number, createdAt: string, gaps: number[] = [], silent: number[] = []) {
    50	  return {
    51	    assignment_id: `assign-${seconds}`,
    52	    attempt_no: 1,
    53	    source_section: "PT105.S2",
    54	    graded_at: createdAt,
    55	    created_at: createdAt,
    56	    stimuli: Array.from({ length: SLOTS }, (_, i) => {
    57	      const n = i + 1;
    58	      if (silent.includes(n)) return stim(n, { spoken: "", carried: 0, percent: 0 });
    59	      if (gaps.includes(n)) return stim(n, { ungraded: "judge returned nothing" });
    60	      return stim(n);

/* Motion is a courtesy, never the mechanism. Every state above is reachable
   with this on; continuous loops stop rather than speed up. */
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .sl *,
  .sl *::before,
  .sl *::after {
    animation: none !important;
    transition: none !important;
  }
  .mw-layer,
  .mw-stage,
  .rd-hero-copy {
    will-change: auto;
  }
}

/* ------------------------------------------------------- the briefing page
   The instructions on their own page, before anything records. One column,
   everything open, Start at the top and again at the foot. */
.sl-briefing-page {
  max-width: 880px;
=== 595,630 ===

  height: 8px;
}

}

  }

  }
}

  height: 1em;
  margin-left: 1px;
  vertical-align: -2px;
  background: var(--rd-apricot-light);
  animation: rd-caret 1s steps(2) infinite;
}

    opacity: 0;
  }
}

command: mkdir -p "C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/fffa8ec5-cd71-482e-a459-d36142834305/scratchpad" && cat > "C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/fffa8ec5-cd71-482e-a459-d36142834305/scratchpad/patch.mjs" <<'PATCHER'
// Applies a JSON edit list. Each edit must match exactly once, or nothing is written.
import fs from "node:fs";
const edits = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(process.argv[2], "utf8"));
const byFile = new Map();
for (const e of edits) {
  if (!byFile.has(e.file)) byFile.set(e.file, fs.readFileSync(e.file, "utf8"));
  let src = byFile.get(e.file);
  const n = src.split(e.old).length - 1;
  if (n !== 1) {
    console.error(`FAIL ${e.file} :: ${n} matches for:\n${e.old.slice(0, 120)}`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  byFile.set(e.file, src.replace(e.old, e.new));
}
for (const [f, src] of byFile) fs.writeFileSync(f, src);
console.log(`ok: ${edits.length} edits across ${byFile.size} files`);

description: Write the marker-delimited patcher
command: SP="C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/fffa8ec5-cd71-482e-a459-d36142834305/scratchpad" && cat > "$SP/patch.mjs" <<'PATCHER'
/**
 * Applies a marker-delimited edit list. Every OLD must match exactly once or
 * nothing is written, so a stale needle fails loudly instead of half-patching.
 *
 *   @@FILE <path>
 *   @@OLD
 *   ...text...
 *   @@NEW
 *   ...text...
 *   @@END
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
const text = fs.readFileSync(process.argv[2], "utf8").replace(/\r\n/g, "\n");
const blocks = text.split("@@FILE ").slice(1);
const byFile = new Map();
let count = 0;
for (const block of blocks) {
  const nl = block.indexOf("\n");
  const file = block.slice(0, nl).trim();
  const body = block.slice(nl + 1);
  const oldStart = body.indexOf("@@OLD\n") + 6;
  const newStart = body.indexOf("\n@@NEW\n");
  const endStart = body.indexOf("\n@@END");
  const oldText = body.slice(oldStart, newStart);
  const newText = body.slice(newStart + 7, endStart);
  if (!byFile.has(file)) byFile.set(file, fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").replace(/\r\n/g, "\n"));
  const src = byFile.get(file);
  const n = src.split(oldText).length - 1;
  if (n !== 1) {
    console.error(`FAIL ${file}: ${n} matches for >>>${oldText.slice(0, 140)}<<<`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  byFile.set(file, src.replace(oldText, newText));
  count++;
}
for (const [f, src] of byFile) fs.writeFileSync(f, src);
console.log(`ok: ${count} edits across ${byFile.size} files`);
PATCHER
echo ok
[/external_agent_tool_call]

export interface MeadowSceneProps {

/**
 * The grass sways in six groups, not in one animation per blade.
 *
 * A keyframe on every `<path>` put 170 animations on the compositor and left
 * them running for as long as the tab was open, for a picture that is often
 * scrolled past. Six groups skewed about the ground line read the same at a
 * glance -- skew, not rotation, because it leans the tips and leaves every
 * base planted -- and cost the browser six. The negative delays start each
 * group part-way through its cycle so they are out of phase on the first
 * frame rather than a beat later.
 */
const SWAY = [
  { delay: -0.0, duration: 5.5 },
  { delay: -1.4, duration: 6.3 },
  { delay: -2.7, duration: 4.9 },
  { delay: -0.7, duration: 7.1 },
  { delay: -3.4, duration: 5.9 },
  { delay: -2.1, duration: 6.7 },
];

export interface MeadowSceneProps {
@@END
@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/MeadowScene.tsx
@@OLD
export function MeadowScene({ progress, children }: MeadowSceneProps) {
  const reduced = useReducedMotion();

  const far = useMemo(() => ridge(11, 545, 42), []);
@@NEW
export function MeadowScene({ progress, children }: MeadowSceneProps) {
  const reduced = useReducedMotion();
  const rootRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  // Nothing here is worth animating while the hero is off screen.
  const inView = useInView(rootRef);

  const far = useMemo(() => ridge(11, 545, 42), []);
@@END
@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/MeadowScene.tsx
@@OLD
  const { blades, flowers } = useMemo(() => grass(71, 170), []);
@@NEW
  const { blades, flowers } = useMemo(() => grass(71, 170), []);
  const bladeGroups = useMemo(() => {
    const groups: Blade[][] = SWAY.map(() => []);
    for (const b of blades) groups[Math.min(SWAY.length - 1, Math.floor((b.delay / 4) * SWAY.length))].push(b);
    return groups;
  }, [blades]);
@@END

@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css
@@OLD
.mw-grain {
  z-index: 5;
  inset: 0;
  height: 100%;
  opacity: 0.13;
  mix-blend-mode: soft-light;
  pointer-events: none;
}
@@NEW
.mw-grain {
  z-index: 5;
  inset: 0;
  height: 100%;
  opacity: 0.13;
  mix-blend-mode: soft-light;
  pointer-events: none;
  /* The grain never moves. `.mw-layer` promises the compositor a transform on
     every plane, which held a full-size turbulence-filtered layer in memory
     for nothing. */
  will-change: auto;
}
@@END
@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css
@@OLD
.mw-blades path {
  animation: mw-sway 5.5s ease-in-out infinite alternate;
}
@keyframes mw-sway {
  from {
    transform: rotate(-1.8deg);
  }
  to {
    transform: rotate(1.8deg);
  }
}
@@NEW
/* Six groups, not 170 blades. The duration and the phase come from the scene,
   which knows how many groups there are; the origin sits on the ground line so
   the skew leans the tips and leaves every base planted. */
.mw-blades g {
  animation: mw-sway 5.5s ease-in-out infinite alternate;
}
@keyframes mw-sway {
  from {
    transform: skewX(-1.6deg);
  }
  to {
    transform: skewX(1.6deg);
  }
}

/* Loops stop when their surface leaves the screen. The hero's scene and its
   demonstration, and the four scenes on the step cards, are the whole of the
   door's continuous motion; off screen they are work nobody can see. */
.mw[data-still] .mw-blades g,
.rd-demo[data-still] .rd-rec-dot,
.rd-demo[data-still] .rd-wave i,
.rd-demo[data-still] .rd-caret,
.rd-method[data-still] .rd-scene *,
.rd-method[data-still] .rd-scene *::before,
.rd-method[data-still] .rd-scene *::after {
  animation-play-state: paused;
}
@@END
@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css
@@OLD
.sl-crumb-back {
  font: inherit;
  letter-spacing: inherit;
  text-transform: inherit;
  color: inherit;
  background: none;
  border: 0;
  padding: 0;
  cursor: pointer;
  text-decoration: underline;
  text-decoration-style: dotted;
  text-underline-offset: 4px;
}
@@NEW
.sl-crumb-back {
  position: relative;
  font: inherit;
  letter-spacing: inherit;
  text-transform: inherit;
  color: inherit;
  background: none;
  border: 0;
  padding: 0;
  cursor: pointer;
  text-decoration: underline;
  text-decoration-style: dotted;
  text-underline-offset: 4px;
}
/* The way out of the drill, drawn at 11px because it is a breadcrumb and
   pressable at 71x45 because it is the exit. The target is an overlay rather
   than padding so the crumb line keeps its height, the same way the runner's
   rail controls were done. */
.sl-crumb-back::after {
  content: "";
  position: absolute;
  inset: -14px -8px;
}
@@END
@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css
@@OLD
.sl-dr-tab {
  appearance: none;
  border: 0;
  background: none;
  cursor: pointer;
  font: inherit;
  font-size: 14px;
  font-weight: 500;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
  padding: 8px 0 14px;
@@NEW
.sl-dr-tab {
  appearance: none;
  border: 0;
  background: none;
  cursor: pointer;
  font: inherit;
  font-size: 14px;
  font-weight: 500;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
  /* 45px tall. It was 43, and the underline still has to land on the tab
     strip's own border, so the height goes on the top. */
  padding: 10px 0 14px;
@@END
@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css
@@OLD
.rd-demo-play {
  appearance: none;
  cursor: pointer;
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  width: 28px;
  height: 28px;
  border-radius: 50%;
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
  background: var(--rd-paper);
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  padding: 0;
  transition: background 0.2s ease;
}
@@NEW
.rd-demo-play {
  appearance: none;
  cursor: pointer;
  position: relative;
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  width: 28px;
  height: 28px;
  border-radius: 50%;
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
  background: var(--rd-paper);
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  padding: 0;
  transition: background 0.2s ease;
}
/* 44x44 to press, 28x28 to look at. The card's chrome is 13px type and a row
   of 9px dots; a 44px disc in it would be the loudest thing on the hero. The
   gap to the clock is 12px, so the overlay reaches into padding and never onto
   a neighbour. */
.rd-demo-play::after {
  content: "";
  position: absolute;
  inset: -8px;
}
/* The hero paints its focus ring white for the dark panel. The demonstration
   is cream paper sitting on that panel, where white measured 1.05:1. */
.rd-hero .rd-demo :focus-visible {
  outline-color: var(--rd-ink);
}
@@END

input: {"action":"javascript_exec","tabId":"seed","text":"\nfunction targetProbe(sel, wantW, wantH) {\n  const el = document.querySelector(sel); if (!el) return {sel, missing:true};\n  el.scrollIntoView({block:'center'});\n  const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();\n  const cx=r.left+r.width/2, cy=r.top+r.height/2;\n  const hw=wantW/2-1, hh=wantH/2-1;                 // 1px inside the wanted target\n  const pts=[[0,0],[-hw,0],[hw,0],[0,-hh],[0,hh],[-hw,-hh],[hw,-hh],[-hw,hh],[hw,hh]];\n  const hits=pts.map(([dx,dy])=>{const t=document.elementFromPoint(cx+dx,cy+dy); return t===el||el.contains(t);});\n  // widen until it stops hitting, to report the real target box\n  let w=0; while(w<80 && document.elementFromPoint(cx+w/2, cy)===el) w+=2;\n  let h=0; while(h<80 && document.elementFromPoint(cx, cy+h/2)===el) h+=2;\n  return {sel, visual:`${Math.round(r.width)}x${Math.round(r.height)}`, wants:`${wantW}x${wantH}`,\n          allNinePointsHit: hits.every(Boolean), measuredTarget:`${w}x${h}`};\n}\nconst out = {\n  viewport: `${innerWidth}x${innerHeight}`,\n  play: targetProbe('.rd-demo-play', 44, 44),\n  crumb: targetProbe('.sl-crumb-back', 44, 44),\n  drawerTabs: [...document.querySelectorAll('.sl-dr-tab')].map(t=>getComputedStyle(t).height + ' x ' + getComputedStyle(t).width),\n};\nwindow.scrollTo(0,0);\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,400));\nout.animationsAtRestTop = document.getAnimations().length;\n// scroll the hero away and confirm the meadow + demo actually stop\nwindow.scrollTo(0, innerHeight*2.2);\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,700));\nout.heroOffScreen = {\n  mwStill: document.querySelector('.mw').hasAttribute('data-still'),\n  demoStill: document.querySelector('.rd-demo').hasAttribute('data-still'),\n  runningAnimations: document.getAnimations().filter(a=>a.playState==='running').length,\n  pausedAnimations: document.getAnimations().filter(a=>a.playState==='paused').length,\n};\nwindow.scrollTo(0,0);\nreturn out;\n"}

description: Add and run the door a11y test
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && cat > src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLaunchFrame.a11y.test.tsx <<'TEST'
import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import instructionsJson from "../content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import { SectionLaunchFrame } from "./SectionLaunchFrame";

/**
 * What the door owes a keyboard and a screen reader.
 *
 * Measured in the browser on 2026-09-09, the door announced its demonstration
 * on a loop, put that demonstration's Play button in front of the way out as
 * the first thing Tab reached, and ran a heading outline of H1, H3, H2. None
 * of that is visible in a screenshot and none of it was under test, so each
 * one is pinned here.
 */
const instructions = instructionsJson as unknown as ActivityInstructions;

function renderDoor(props: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionLaunchFrame>[0]> = {}) {
  return render(
    <SectionLaunchFrame
      title="Read, Recall, Colour · PT105 Section 2"
      subtitle="Say it back."
      instructions={instructions}
      onExit={() => {}}
      {...props}
    >
      <div>the sections</div>
    </SectionLaunchFrame>,
  );
}

const LEVEL = (h: Element) => Number(h.tagName[1]);

describe("the door's outline, focus order and live regions", () => {
  it("starts at h1 and never skips a level on the way down", () => {
    const { container } = renderDoor();
    const levels = [...container.querySelectorAll("h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6")].map(LEVEL);

    expect(levels[0]).toBe(1);
    for (let i = 1; i < levels.length; i++) {
      expect(levels[i] - levels[i - 1]).toBeLessThanOrEqual(1);
    }
    // Every section down the page owns a heading, not just an aria-label.
    expect(levels.filter((l) => l === 2).length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(3);
  });

  it("keeps the outline intact with the full instructions open", async () => {
    const { container } = renderDoor();
    screen.getByRole("tab", { name: /full instructions/i }).click();

    const levels = [...container.querySelectorAll("h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6")].map(LEVEL);
    for (let i = 1; i < levels.length; i++) {
      expect(levels[i] - levels[i - 1]).toBeLessThanOrEqual(1);
    }
  });

  it("never announces the demonstration, which turns over every few seconds", () => {
    const { container } = renderDoor();
    expect(container.querySelectorAll(".rd-demo [aria-live]")).toHaveLength(0);
  });

  it("puts the way out before the demonstration's Play button", () => {
    const { container } = renderDoor();
    const focusable = [...container.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>("a[href],button,[tabindex]:not([tabindex='-1'])")];

    expect(focusable[0]).toHaveClass("sl-crumb-back");
    const play = focusable.findIndex((el) => el.classList.contains("rd-demo-play"));
    const exit = focusable.findIndex((el) => el.classList.contains("sl-crumb-back"));
    expect(play).toBeGreaterThan(exit);
  });

  it("holds every loop still until its surface is on screen", () => {
    // The IntersectionObserver stub in the test setup never reports a crossing,
    // which is exactly the off-screen case: nothing should be running.
    const { container } = renderDoor();
    expect(container.querySelector(".mw")).toHaveAttribute("data-still");
    expect(container.querySelector(".rd-demo")).toHaveAttribute("data-still");
    expect(container.querySelector(".rd-method")).toHaveAttribute("data-still");
  });
});
TEST

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
**WCAG 2.1 AA is a binding floor across every surface.** It is a defect gate, not an aspiration. A finding below the AA bar is a bug and is fixed, not argued.

- Text contrast is at least 4.5:1 against its actual rendered background, and at least 3:1 for large text and for the visual boundaries of interactive controls. Students read dense argument text on this platform for hours at a time.
- Every interactive element has a visible focus indicator.
- `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected. Motion is never load-bearing for understanding or for completing a task.
- Touch targets are 44x44px, and meet it by being that size rather than by a side effect such as wrapped text. This one is a house standard above the floor, not part of it: 44x44 is WCAG 2.5.5, which is Level AAA. The binding target-size rule is 2.5.8 at 24x24, so a control that clears 24x24 on a fine pointer clears the floor and is not a defect.
- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.
- Contrast is verified against what actually renders in the browser, not against the token values a theme is assumed to apply. The theme layer has shipped a light card on a near-black page while the token file read as correct.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]

command: SP="C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/fffa8ec5-cd71-482e-a459-d36142834305/scratchpad" && cat > "$SP/product.patch" <<'PATCH'

@@OLD
- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.
@@NEW
- A dense row of marks that exists to be read at a glance is the one place the 44x44 house standard yields. The Report Card's ledger is 22 to 26 ticks inside an 820px card; at 44px wide they would need 968 to 1144px, so meeting the standard means scrolling or wrapping, and the ledger stops being a single picture of the run. They are 44 tall and 28 to 30 wide, they clear 2.5.8, and that is the settled answer rather than an open defect. Joshua's call, 2026-09-09.
- Where a control has to stay small because it sits in chrome set in 11 or 13px type, the 44x44 target may be an explicit transparent overlay centred on the control instead of the control's own box. An overlay is a target somebody designed and it is proved by hit-testing the four edges; wrapped text is an accident and still does not count. The runner's rail controls, the door's crumb out and the demonstration's Play button are all built this way.
- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.
@@END
PATCH
node "$SP/patch.mjs" "$SP/product.patch"
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## assistant

On mobile `.rd-hero-copy` was sitting over the lower half of the crumb's target. Fixing the layer order:

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Raise the crumb row above the hero copy
command: SP="C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/fffa8ec5-cd71-482e-a459-d36142834305/scratchpad" && cat > "$SP/css3.patch" <<'PATCH'
@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css
@@OLD
.rd-hero-top {
  position: relative;
  z-index: 8;
@@NEW
.rd-hero-top {
  position: relative;
  /* Above the hero copy, which shares the same layer and comes later in the
     DOM. On a phone the copy block starts level with the crumb line and took
     the bottom half of the way out with it: a 73x47 target on a laptop
     measured 73x32 at 375px. Nothing decorative sits over the exit. */
  z-index: 9;
@@END
@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css.test.ts
@@OLD
    const tab = CSS.match(/\.sl-dr-tab\s*\{([^}]*)\}/)?.[1] ?? "";
    expect(tab).toMatch(/padding:\s*10px 0 14px/);
  });
@@NEW
    const tab = CSS.match(/\.sl-dr-tab\s*\{([^}]*)\}/)?.[1] ?? "";
    expect(tab).toMatch(/padding:\s*10px 0 14px/);
  });

  it("keeps the way out above the hero copy", () => {
    // Same layer, and the copy comes later in the DOM, so on a phone it took
    // the bottom half of the crumb's target with it.
    const z = (sel: string) => Number(CSS.match(new RegExp(`\\${sel}\\s*\\{[^}]*z-index:\\s*(\\d+)`))?.[1]);
    expect(z(".rd-hero-top")).toBeGreaterThan(z(".rd-hero-copy"));
  });
@@END
PATCH
node "$SP/patch.mjs" "$SP/css3.patch"
[/external_agent_tool_call]

**The authored rubric named the wrong half.** `coach.rubric[6]` read "Score The Recall against the captured key for that stimulus", which points the memory step at the reasoning key. ADR 0025 quotes that same sentence as "Score The Read". Restored. Nothing live was mis-graded: `grade-rrc-section` builds its own prompt and never reads `coach.rubric`, so the judge and `BAND_FLOOR` (ADR 0035) are untouched and no census re-run is needed.

## Step 6: done, 2026-09-09

Commit `53dea8a`, "RRC: the door stops running for nobody, and the exit comes first". On branch `rrc-step-6`, [PR #101](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/101), not on `main`. Measured on the harness at 1280x900 and again at 375x812, before and after.

### What the door was spending

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Animations running at rest, top of page | 187 | 19 |
| Animations running once the hero is scrolled past | 187 | 0 from the hero |
| Blade animations | 170, one per `<path>` | 6, one per group |
| Headline scroll blur | `blur(14px)` | `blur(4px)` |
| `will-change` on the static grain layer | `transform` | `auto` |

The grass now sways by `skewX` about the ground line instead of rotating each blade about its own base. Rotating six groups would have lifted the far blades off the ground line; skew leans the tips and leaves every base planted. Verified: the group transform is `matrix(1, 0, ±0.028, 1, 0, 0)`, base drift 0px, tip vertical drift 0px. The picture is unchanged. Blades per group: 33, 33, 29, 27, 26, 22, on six durations from 4.9s to 7.1s with negative delays so they are out of phase on the first frame.

Gating is a `data-still` attribute driven by `useInView` on three roots: `.mw`, `.rd-demo` and `.rd-method`. The demonstration's `setTimeout`, `setInterval` and per-frame typewriter stop with it and pick up from the beat they were on.

### What it owed a keyboard

- The live region is gone from the demonstration's step label. It turned over every 1.5 to 4.2 seconds for as long as the door was open.
- The scene moved last in the DOM. It is painted by z-index, so nothing moved visually, and the first four tab stops are now Library, Start the section, Read the full instructions, then the demo's Play button. Play used to be first, ahead of the way out.
- Targets, proved by hit-testing all four edges at both widths: Play 28x28 to **44x44**, crumb 55x17 to **73x47**, drawer tabs 43px tall to **58x47**. Drawn sizes unchanged; the targets are explicit overlays, the same way Step 4 did the runner's rail.
- `.rd-hero-top` lifted above `.rd-hero-copy`. They shared a layer and the copy came later in the DOM, so at 375px it sat over the bottom half of the exit's target: 73x32 instead of 73x47. Found only because the phone width was checked separately.
- The demo's focus ring was the hero's white on cream paper at 1.05:1. It is ink now, scoped to the card.
- The outline ran H1, H3, H2, and H1 then six H4s with the drawer open. Every section down the page now owns a level-2 heading carrying the exact string its `aria-label` already carried, so no accessible name changed, and the essay headings moved to H3.

### A third correction to this note's own audit

**The two gradient-stop contrast failures do not exist.** `section-launch.css:843` and `:1575` were reported from the rendered detector scan. The detector compared each text colour against its container's terminal gradient stop without knowing where in the box the text actually sits. Measured positionally in the browser:

| Text | Detector | Actually sits on | Measured |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tell diorama caption | 2.4:1 on `#a89fe0` | `rgb(62,54,106)` at 29% down | **10.82:1** |
| Closing panel line | 2.0:1 on `#a89fe0` | `rgb(42,36,79)` at 37% down | **14.35:1** |
| Its emphasis | | `rgb(47,40,86)` | **11.31:1** |
| Closing panel button | | its own opaque `#f6f3ea` chip | **16.1:1** |

Nothing is drawn on the pale end of either gradient, and the tell diorama's bubble paints its own opaque `#1a1635` regardless. No change made. The lesson repeats Step 2's: a finding taken from a record or a tool rather than from the rendered pixel can send a fix at something that was never wrong. Darkening those gradients would have flattened the dusk for nothing.

### Guards

- New `SectionLaunchFrame.a11y.test.tsx`, 5 assertions: the outline starts at H1 and skips no level, open and closed; no live region in the demonstration; the exit precedes the Play button; every loop still until its surface is on screen.
- `section-launch.css.test.ts` gains 6: grouped sway rather than per-blade, skew rather than rotation, the three `data-still` pause rules, the grain's `will-change`, the scoped ink focus ring, the three overlay targets, and the hero layer order.

### Verification

105 test files, 2778 tests, all passing. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

### Records

`PRODUCT.md` gains the two target-size rulings this needed. Dense glanceable rows are where the 44x44 house standard yields, which settles the ledger question above. A deliberate transparent overlay is a real target, proved by hit-testing, where wrapped text still is not.

### Separate branches, at last

Joshua's call, 2026-09-09: one branch per session. This step is the first to use it. Nothing here can reach production on the other session's push, which is what happened to Step 5. The working tree is still shared, so a branch protects this session and not the other one; a worktree would be the fuller fix if both sessions keep running together.

Next: Step 7, the handover and the numbers. The numeracy policy is already decided: percentages are allowed per stimulus and at section level, said identically on the door and the card, with "Recalled" renamed to "Recorded".
@@END

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
---
name: push-only-your-own-commits
description: "Never push commits this session did not make; leave another session's or Lovable's unpushed work alone."
metadata: 
  node_type: memory
  type: feedback
  originSessionId: 810e4352-f17a-4f97-96c4-7e4935eb07bb
  modified: 2026-09-09T09:43:59.339Z
---

Push only the commits this chat made itself. If `git log origin/main..HEAD` contains
commits authored by another Claude session, another chat, or a Lovable sync, do not push
them. Stick to your own work, say what you are leaving behind, and stop, unless Joshua
asks you to push the rest.

**Why:** several sessions and Lovable write to `main` in the same repo at the same time.
Pushing a commit whose author has not chosen to publish it takes that decision away from
them, and on this project a push is the first half of a deploy, so it can carry
half-finished work toward production. Set on 2026-09-09 after this session ran
`.claude/safe-push.sh` and the push went out as `e1b2507..5fd1074`, carrying another
session's `70056d3` along with its own commit.

**How to apply:** before `bash .claude/safe-push.sh`, run `git log --oneline
origin/main..HEAD` and confirm every listed commit is one you wrote this session. If any
is not, do not push. This compounds with the existing rule never to `git add -A` in this
repo: a broad add sweeps up other people's in-flight edits, and a broad push then
publishes them. That already happened in reverse here, when a concurrent session's
`git add` swept this session's uncommitted motion work into its own commit.

Related: [[lovable-frontend-publish-is-separate]], [[lsatu-content-lives-in-postgres]].
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Record the branch-per-session rule
command: M="C:/Users/Joshu/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory" && cat > "$M/branch-per-session-in-lsatu.md" <<'MEM'
---
name: branch-per-session-in-lsatu
description: "Concurrent agent sessions each work on their own branch off main in projects/lsatu and open a PR; never commit straight to main."
metadata:
  type: feedback
---

In `projects/lsatu`, when more than one agent session may be running, work on a named
branch off `main` (`git checkout -b <topic>`) and open a PR. Do not commit to `main`.

**Why:** Joshua's call, 2026-09-09, after two sessions committing to `main` in the same
working tree pushed each other's work. One session's push carried the other's commit; the
other's push carried this session's plus two untested commits, which then went to
production because a Lovable deploy ships `main`. Every commit is authored "Joshua
Fisseha", so authorship cannot separate the sessions. He chose branch-per-session over
worktrees.

**How to apply:** branch before the first edit, commit with explicit paths (never `git
add -A`, the tree holds other sessions' uncommitted files), push the branch, open a PR
with `gh pr create --base main`. Check `git branch --show-current` before each commit: the
working tree is still shared, so a branch protects your commits and not the other
session's, and a peer that believes it is on `main` will commit onto your branch. If both
sessions will run for a while, a `git worktree` is the fuller fix and is worth raising.

**Step 6: door accessibility and performance**

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Animations running at rest | 187 | 19 |
| …once the hero is scrolled past | 187 | 0 from the hero |
| Blade animations | 170 (one per path) | 6 (one per group) |
| Play button target | 28×28 | 44×44 |
| Crumb "Library" target | 55×17 | 73×47 |
| Drawer tabs | 43px tall | 58×47 |
| Heading outline | H1 → H3 → H2 | H1 → H2 → H3, no skips |

The grass sways by `skewX` about the ground line instead of rotating each blade about its own base — verified `matrix(1, 0, ±0.028, 1, 0, 0)`, base drift 0px, tip vertical drift 0px, so the picture is unchanged. The demo's `aria-live` is gone (it announced itself every 1.5–4.2s forever), the scene moved last in the DOM so the exit is the first Tab stop instead of the Play button, and the demo's focus ring went from white-on-cream at 1.05:1 to ink.

One thing I found only by checking 375px separately: `.rd-hero-copy` shared a layer with the crumb row and came later in the DOM, so on a phone it sat over the bottom half of the exit's target (73×32, not 73×47). Fixed.

**A correction to the audit.** The two "gradient-stop contrast failures" don't exist. The detector compared each text colour against its container's *terminal* gradient stop without knowing where the text sits. Measured positionally: the tell diorama's caption is 10.82:1 (and its bubble paints its own opaque indigo anyway), the closing panel's line is 14.35:1, its emphasis 11.31:1, and the button below is an opaque cream chip at 16.1:1. Nothing is drawn on the pale end of either gradient. Darkening those gradients would have flattened the dusk for nothing, so I made no change and wrote it up.

.ac-section-ready-retry,
.ac-section-ready-leave {
  border-radius: 9999px;
  padding: 0.4rem 1.1rem;

@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {

/* ---- leaving the drill room -------------------------------------------- */

/* The one composed moment in the drill.
 *
 * The runner is a cold indigo mirror of the real section; the Report Card is
 * warm paper. The jump between them was abrupt because there was nothing
 * between them: the field cut to the card in one frame, at the end of
 * twenty-five unbroken minutes, which is the moment she is most likely to
 * remember and the moment that felt most like a page reload.
 *
 * So the field lifts rather than cuts. The paper card holds its position and
 * fades with it, the indigo warms to the ground the Report Card sits on, and
 * the handover happens underneath. Nothing waits on it: the save, the upload
 * and the grading request all run through these nine hundred milliseconds, and
 * the transition is over long before any of them are.
 */
.ac-section.is-finishing {
  /* The Activity Centre's own canvas, by value rather than by eye.
     `--background` is scoped to `.activity-centre`, which this element is not
     inside, and landing two shades off it turns a handover into a visible step
     at the exact moment it is meant to be invisible. Kept beside the token it
     copies so a change to one is a change to look for in the other. */
  background: hsl(38 36% 93%);
  transition: background 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
}

/** Her last few runs, and what each one covered. */
function RunHistory({ state }: { state: HistoryState }) {
  if (state.kind === "idle") return null;

  return (
    <div className="sl-runs">
      <div>
        <span className="sl-label">
          {state.kind === "ready" && state.runs.length > 0
            ? state.runs.length === 1
              ? "Your first run"
              : `Your last ${state.runs.length} runs`
            : "Your recent runs"}
        </span>
        {state.kind === "loading" && (
          <p className="sl-note" style={{ marginTop: 10 }}>
            <span className="sl-skel" style={{ display: "block", width: "70%" }} />
          </p>
        )}
        {state.kind === "failed" && (
          <p className="sl-note" style={{ marginTop: 10 }}>
            Your run history is not available right now. The section still starts
            normally.
          </p>
        )}
        {state.kind === "ready" && state.runs.length === 0 && (
          <p className="sl-note" style={{ marginTop: 10 }}>
            No runs yet. The first one is the baseline, not the test.
          </p>
        )}
        {state.kind === "ready" && state.runs.length > 0 && (
          <div className="sl-scroll">
            <table className="sl-rtable">
              <thead>
                <tr>
                  <th>Date</th>
                  <th>Time</th>
                  <th>Recalled</th>
                  <th className="sl-r">Stimulus by stimulus</th>
                </tr>
              </thead>
              <tbody>
                {state.runs.map((run) => {

234 recall/runStats.ts
import type { RecentSectionRun } from "../api/activitiesClient";

/**
 * What the launch surface says about a student's last few runs, derived and
 * nothing else.
 *
 * Every number here is read off runs she has already done. Nothing is scored,
 * nothing is predicted, and a number that cannot be established honestly is
 * absent rather than estimated -- which is why several of these are nullable.
 * The rule the whole file obeys: if we cannot know it, we do not print it.
 */

/**
 * One stimulus slot in a run, as the tick row draws it.
 *
 * Three states, not two, and the third is the important one. `notJudged` is a
 * stimulus she recorded and the judge returned no verdict for: our outage, not
 * her miss. Drawing it as an empty slot would tell her she skipped work she
 * actually did.
 *
 * `silent` is the fourth, and it is the one that is easy to get wrong. A
 * stimulus she never spoke into still produces a full grade row: the grader
 * writes `spoken: ""` with zero coverage and NO `ungraded` flag
 * (`grade-rrc-section/index.ts`). Treating the presence of a row as evidence of
 * recall would count her silence as work done and report "25 of 25" to somebody
 * who said nothing.
 *
 * The names deliberately avoid `carried`, which already means something else on
 * `SectionStimulusGrade` -- the NUMBER of details retained. Reusing it for "a
 * verdict exists" would make the two readings collide.
 */
export type TickState = "recorded" | "notJudged" | "silent" | "missing";

export interface RunTicks {
  /** One entry per authored slot, in authored order. */
  ticks: TickState[];
  /** Stimuli she actually spoke into, judged or not. Silence is not counted. */
  recorded: number;
  /** Authored slots in the section. */
  slots: number;
}

export interface SectionStats {
  /** Seconds on the clock for the most recent run. */
  lastRunSeconds: number | null;
  /**
   * Newest minus oldest, in seconds, across the runs shown. Negative is faster.
   * Null with fewer than two runs, where there is no trend to state.
   */
  trendSeconds: number | null;
  /** How many runs the trend spans. */
  trendRuns: number;
  /** Stimuli recorded across every run whose slot count is knowable. */
  recalled: number | null;
  /** Authored slots across those same runs. */
  recalledOf: number | null;
  /** The clock this section is run against, from the assignment. */
  targetMinutes: number | null;
  /** Runs finished inside the clock, of the ones measured against THIS target. */
  metTarget: number;
[/external_agent_tool_result]

): SectionStats {
  const empty: SectionStats = {
    lastRunSeconds: null,
    trendSeconds: null,
    trendRuns: 0,
    recalled: null,
    recalledOf: null,
    targetMinutes,
    metTarget: 0,
    targetRuns: 0,
    totalRuns: 0,
  };
  if (runs.length === 0) return empty;

  const newest = seconds(runs[0]);
  const oldest = seconds(runs[runs.length - 1]);

  // Newest minus oldest. Getting faster has to come out negative, and the
  // subtraction the other way round reports an improving student as slowing
  // down.
  const trendSeconds =
    runs.length >= 2 && newest !== null && oldest !== null ? newest - oldest : null;

  // Only runs whose slot count is knowable contribute. A run with an unknowable
  // denominator is left out of both halves of the fraction rather than being
  // given a smaller one, which would inflate the percentage.
  let recalled = 0;
  let recalledOf = 0;
  let counted = 0;
  for (const run of runs) {
    const t = ticksFor(run);
    if (!t) continue;
    recalled += t.recorded;
    recalledOf += t.slots;
    counted += 1;
  }

  /*
   * The target cell is labelled with a CLOCK, so it has to count the clock.
   *
   * `within_goal` is stricter than it looks: it means inside the clock AND every
   * stimulus coloured and given a Read. Counting it under "25:00" would mark a
   * run that finished in twenty-two minutes as missing a twenty-five minute
   * target because of something that is not time at all. `within_time` is the
   * flag that means what the label says; older rows predate it, so the clock is
   * recomputed for those.
   *
   * Only runs measured against the SAME target are counted. A run from before an
   * accommodation changed was judged against a different number, and folding it
   * in reports her against a clock she was never given.
   */
  const comparable = runs.filter(
    (r) => targetMinutes !== null && r.section?.goal_minutes === targetMinutes,
  );
  const insideClock = (r: RecentSectionRun) =>
    typeof r.section?.within_time === "boolean"
      ? r.section.within_time
      : r.section.total_seconds <= r.section.goal_minutes * 60;

  return {
    lastRunSeconds: newest,
    trendSeconds,
    trendRuns: runs.length,
    recalled: counted > 0 ? recalled : null,
    recalledOf: counted > 0 ? recalledOf : null,
    targetMinutes,
    metTarget: comparable.filter(insideClock).length,
    targetRuns: comparable.length,
    totalRuns: runs.length,
  };
}

/** `1502` -> `25:02`. */
export function clock(totalSeconds: number): string {
  const sign = totalSeconds < 0 ? "-" : "";
  const s = Math.abs(Math.round(totalSeconds));
  return `${sign}${Math.floor(s / 60)}:${String(s % 60).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

/** The trend, signed for a reader: `-6:52` is six minutes and change faster. */
export function signedClock(totalSeconds: number): string {
  if (totalSeconds === 0) return "0:00";
  const body = clock(Math.abs(totalSeconds));
  return `${totalSeconds < 0 ? "−" : "+"}${body}`;
}

.rc {
  /* ── The console: a deep plum field ─────────────────────────────────── */
  --rc-console: oklch(0.245 0.058 312);

  --rc-console-ink-dim: oklch(0.815 0.035 312);
  --rc-console-ink-faint: oklch(0.7 0.05 312);

  /* ── The page: a violet-cast white, never cream, never grey ─────────── */
  --rc-page: oklch(0.979 0.008 312);
  --rc-card: oklch(0.995 0.004 312);
  --rc-fill: oklch(0.958 0.012 312);
  --rc-ink: oklch(0.235 0.032 312);
  --rc-ink-muted: oklch(0.475 0.03 312);
  --rc-ink-faint: oklch(0.505 0.035 312);
  --rc-hairline: oklch(0.9 0.014 312);
  --rc-hairline-soft: oklch(0.945 0.011 312);

  /* ── The four categories. Each is a KIND of feedback, not a severity. ─
     `-ink` is the text weight, measured to clear 4.5:1 on both the page and
     its own soft field; `-soft` is that field; `-lit` is the console version,
     which has to clear the same bar against a dark plum instead. */
  --rc-detail-ink: oklch(0.475 0.135 12);
  --rc-detail-soft: oklch(0.952 0.028 12);
  --rc-detail-lit: oklch(0.775 0.115 14);

  --rc-read-ink: oklch(0.46 0.095 218);
  --rc-read-soft: oklch(0.948 0.03 218);
  --rc-read-lit: oklch(0.79 0.1 210);

  --rc-colour-ink: oklch(0.475 0.105 72);
  --rc-colour-soft: oklch(0.955 0.04 82);
  --rc-colour-lit: oklch(0.83 0.13 82);

  --rc-method-ink: oklch(0.455 0.09 278);
  --rc-method-soft: oklch(0.951 0.026 278);
  --rc-method-lit: oklch(0.79 0.085 280);

  /* Sound. Not a category, and the only thing on the page that moves. Held at
     328 rather than nearer the reds: the playhead lands on the same line as the
     dropped-Detail chips, and twenty degrees of hue is not enough separation
     between "this is playing" and "this is what you lost". */
  --rc-signal: oklch(0.545 0.225 328);
  --rc-signal-bright: oklch(0.715 0.2 328);
  --rc-signal-soft: oklch(0.957 0.032 328);
  --rc-signal-ink: oklch(0.995 0.005 328);

  /* The two faint inks are set where they are because that is where they clear
     4.5:1 on their own ground, measured in the browser rather than assumed.
     Every label on this card is 10px, so there is no large-text exemption to
     lean on. */
  --rc-display: "Fraunces", "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;
  --rc-body: "Lexend", "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;
  --rc-mono: "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace;

  /* Exponential ease. No bounce anywhere on this page: it is a page about
     being told what you got wrong, and a spring reads as a toy. */
  --rc-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);

  background: var(--rc-page);
  color: var(--rc-ink);
  font-family: var(--rc-body);
}

/* The card is read on a laptop in a long sitting, so the body column is capped
   at a measure rather than at the shell's container width. */
.rc__measure {
  margin-inline: auto;
  max-width: 72ch;

 * The wait between finishing a section and seeing the Report Card.
 *
 * Grading is a post-section job: one transcription of ~25 minutes of audio plus

 * this tab staying open.
 *

 * a row is the pending signal (ADR 0019): there is no status column to trust,
 * and none she could set herself. If it never arrives, the honest thing to show
 * is that it is still being marked, not an error and certainly not a zero. The
 * sweeper picks up anything this tab missed.

 * repeat after a decent wait recovers all three.

 * repeat arrived while the first grading was still transcribing, nothing was on

          * where it goes.
          */}

          {stillWaiting && (

          */}
        {stillWaiting && (
          <p className="mt-4 text-xs text-muted-foreground">
            If it is still here tomorrow, tell Joshua and quote this run:{" "}
            <code>{assignmentId}</code>.
          </p>
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

/**
 * How much of it came back, in words.
 *
 * A Detail is OUR unit. It is how the judge is asked to mark, it is what the
 * coverage percentage is computed from, and a student has never seen one
 * enumerated: she read a passage and said it back. Printing "20 of 21 details"
 * hands her our machinery and asks her to care about the one number on the page
 * she cannot check (Joshua, 2026-09-08). The band says how it went; this says
 * how much of it she kept, the way a person would say it.
 */
function howMuch(percent: number): string {
  if (percent >= 100) return "All of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 90) return "Almost all of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 75) return "Most of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 55) return "About half of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 25) return "Some of it came back.";
  return "Not much of it came back.";
}

/**
 * Was the run inside its clock?
 *
 * One derivation, from the two numbers the Report Card prints, used everywhere
 * the answer is stated. Three surfaces used to answer this question and they
 * did not have to agree:
 *
 * - The card read the stored `within_time` flag. Where a row predates the flag
 *   it is `undefined`, which is neither true nor false, so the card said
 *   nothing at all and a student got a time with no verdict beside it.
 * - The launch surface preferred the flag and recomputed only when it was
 *   missing, so the same run could be counted one way in the stat strip and
 *   read another way on the card.
 * - Nothing anywhere checked the flag against the figure printed above it, so a
 *   row whose flag and clock disagree renders "28:32" under a twenty-five
 *   minute goal and says "Inside the time".
 *
 * The grader writes `within_time` from exactly this comparison
 * (`grade-rrc-section/index.ts`), so for every run graded since the flag
 * existed, deriving and reading agree. Deriving costs nothing and makes the
 * disagreement unrepresentable: the verdict is a function of the numbers she
 * can see, so it cannot contradict them.
 *
 * `within_goal` is a different and stricter question -- inside the clock AND
 * every stimulus coloured and given a Read -- and stays on the grade, because
 * it is not derivable from the clock alone.
 */
export interface RunClock {
  /** Drill seconds. Pauses are already out of it: the clock stops with the recorder. */
  total_seconds: number;
  /** The clock this section was set, in minutes. */
  goal_minutes: number;
}

export function insideTime(section: RunClock): boolean {
  return section.total_seconds > 0 && section.total_seconds <= section.goal_minutes * 60;
}
TS

[external_agent_tool_result]
/* ── The ground the card lands on ─────────────────────────────────────────
   The runner's report phase is a `.activity-centre` page, which paints the
   Centre's warm cream (`38 36% 93%`). A violet card floating on warm cream is
   the exact clash the palette above was chosen to get away from, and the
   student never sees the card on the neutral ground it was designed against.
   So the report phase brings its own ground, tuned one step darker than the
   card's page so the card still reads as a card. */
/* Compounded rather than bare: `.activity-centre` paints its cream at the same
   specificity, so a lone `.rc-ground` wins or loses on stylesheet order, which
   is not a thing to leave to chance across a code-split build. */
.activity-centre.rc-ground,
.rc-ground {
  background: oklch(0.925 0.014 312);
}

/* ── The focus ring ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   `.activity-centre :focus-visible` forces an emerald ring on every focusable
   thing inside the module. Emerald is precisely what this card must not have,
   and it was reaching the ledger, the transport and every "Hear it" button.
   Same shape as the module's ring, in the colour that means sound here. */
.activity-centre .rc :focus-visible,
.rc :focus-visible {
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 2px var(--rc-page),
    0 0 0 4px var(--rc-signal);
  outline: none;
}

/* On the console the ring has to clear a dark plum instead of paper. */
.activity-centre .rc__console :focus-visible,
.rc__console :focus-visible {
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 2px var(--rc-console),
    0 0 0 4px var(--rc-signal-bright);
}

/* The disclosures ("The passage", the per-movement openers).
 *
 * A `summary` styled as a 10px label is a 16px target. It cannot grow its box
 * without moving the prose it introduces, so it takes the same overlay as

@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/recall/section.css
@@OLD
 * The runner is a cold indigo mirror of the real section; the Report Card is
 * warm paper. The jump between them was abrupt because there was nothing
 * between them: the field cut to the card in one frame, at the end of
 * twenty-five unbroken minutes, which is the moment she is most likely to
 * remember and the moment that felt most like a page reload.
 *
 * So the field lifts rather than cuts. The paper card holds its position and
 * fades with it, the indigo warms to the ground the Report Card sits on, and
 * the handover happens underneath. Nothing waits on it: the save, the upload
 * and the grading request all run through these nine hundred milliseconds, and
 * the transition is over long before any of them are.
 */
.ac-section.is-finishing {
  /* The Activity Centre's own canvas, by value rather than by eye.
     `--background` is scoped to `.activity-centre`, which this element is not
     inside, and landing two shades off it turns a handover into a visible step
     at the exact moment it is meant to be invisible. Kept beside the token it
     copies so a change to one is a change to look for in the other. */
  background: hsl(38 36% 93%);
  transition: background 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
}
@@NEW
 * The runner is a cold indigo mirror of the real section; the Report Card is a
 * violet-cast white on a violet ground. The jump between them was abrupt
 * because there was nothing between them: the field cut to the card in one
 * frame, at the end of twenty-five unbroken minutes, which is the moment she is
 * most likely to remember and the moment that felt most like a page reload.
 *
 * So the field lifts rather than cuts. The paper card holds its position and
 * fades with it, the indigo warms to the ground the Report Card sits on, and
 * the handover happens underneath. Nothing waits on it: the save, the upload
 * and the grading request all run through these nine hundred milliseconds, and
 * the transition is over long before any of them are.
 */
.ac-section.is-finishing {
  /* The ground the card lands on, by value rather than by eye.
     `.rc-ground` (report-card.css) is what the report phase paints, one step
     darker than the card's own `--rc-page` so the card still reads as a card.
     Neither is in scope here, so the value is copied and kept beside the name
     it copies: a change to one is a change to look for in the other.
     It used to warm to the Activity Centre's cream, rgb(244,239,231), which is
     a colour nothing downstream of this moment paints. The field warmed to one
     ground and the card arrived on another, which is the visible step this
     transition exists to remove. */
  background: oklch(0.925 0.014 312);
  transition: background 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
}
@@END

@@NEW
     * The Report Card replaces this screen the moment the save finishes, and

   * It used to be an Activity Centre panel: the Centre's cream surface, the
   * Centre's border and its muted ink, sitting on the violet ground the report
   * phase paints for the Report Card. So the ten seconds after twenty-five
   * unbroken minutes crossed three grounds -- the runner's indigo, a warm
   * cream panel, then the card -- and the last thing she saw before being told
   * how it went was a colour that belonged to neither surface. Inside `.rc` it
   * is the same violet-cast white the card is, on the same ground, and the
   * card replacing it is a change of content rather than a change of place.
   */
  return (
    <div className="rc mx-auto max-w-2xl overflow-hidden rounded-[28px] border border-[var(--rc-hairline)] p-8 text-center shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)] md:p-12">

import { describe, expect, it, vi, beforeEach, afterEach } from "vitest";
import { act, fireEvent, render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { MemoryRouter } from "react-router-dom";
import type { SectionGrade } from "../api/types";
import { SectionReport } from "./SectionReport";

/*
 * The screen navigates now.
 *
 * A Report Card can start a fresh Attempt built from this run's weakest
 * stimuli, and that lands the student in the runner, so `SectionReport` calls
 * `useNavigate` and cannot be rendered outside a Router.
 */
const inRouter = (ui: React.ReactElement) => render(<MemoryRouter>{ui}</MemoryRouter>);

const getSectionGrade = vi.fn();
const requestSectionGrading = vi.fn((_id: string) => Promise.resolve());
vi.mock("../api/activitiesClient", () => ({
  getSectionGrade: (id: string) => getSectionGrade(id),
  requestSectionGrading: (id: string) => requestSectionGrading(id),
  // Reached by the card this screen hands over to. A section nobody has been
  // graded on has none of these, which is also what the database returns.
  listModelRuns: () => Promise.resolve({}),
  signSectionRecording: () => Promise.resolve(null),
}));

// The wait between finishing and seeing the Report Card. Everything here is
// about a failure the student would otherwise experience as "the app ate my
// twenty minutes": a lost grading request, or a slow one shown as an error.

const grade: SectionGrade = {
  assignment_id: "a1",

node "$SP/patch.mjs" "$SP/s7m.patch" && cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && cat > src/features/activity-centre/recall/section.css.test.ts <<'TEST'
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

/**
 * The handover warms to the ground the card actually lands on.
 *
 * `.ac-section.is-finishing` copies a colour it cannot reference: the report
 * phase's ground is declared in `report-card.css`, scoped to elements the
 * runner is not inside. A copied value drifts silently, and this one already
 * had: the field warmed to the Activity Centre's cream while the card arrived
 * on a violet ground, so the ten seconds after twenty-five unbroken minutes
 * crossed three grounds instead of one.
 */
const HERE = __dirname;
const SECTION = fs.readFileSync(path.join(HERE, "section.css"), "utf8");
const REPORT_CARD = fs.readFileSync(path.join(HERE, "../components/report-card.css"), "utf8");

/** The `background:` of the first rule whose selector list contains `selector`. */
function backgroundOf(css: string, selector: string): string {
  const at = css.indexOf(selector);
  const open = css.indexOf("{", at);
  const body = css.slice(open, css.indexOf("}", open));
  return (body.match(/background:\s*([^;]+);/) ?? [])[1]?.trim() ?? "";
}

describe("the handover out of the drill room", () => {
  it("warms to the same colour the report phase paints", () => {
    const warmsTo = backgroundOf(SECTION, ".ac-section.is-finishing");
    const landsOn = backgroundOf(REPORT_CARD, ".rc-ground");
    expect(warmsTo).not.toBe("");
    expect(warmsTo).toBe(landsOn);
  });

  it("does not warm to the Activity Centre's cream, which nothing downstream paints", () => {
    expect(backgroundOf(SECTION, ".ac-section.is-finishing")).not.toMatch(/38\s+36%\s+93%/);
  });
});

## assistant

Caught a visual bug I introduced: `.rc` paints `--rc-page` on the outer square div, so the page colour would show past the panel's rounded corners. Moving the panel chrome onto the element that paints:

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Put the panel chrome on the painting element
command: SP="C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/fffa8ec5-cd71-482e-a459-d36142834305/scratchpad" && cat > "$SP/s7o.patch" <<'PATCH'
@@FILE C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReport.tsx
@@OLD
    <div className="rc mx-auto max-w-2xl">
      {/* No background of its own: `.rc` paints `--rc-page`, which is the card's
          own surface, so the panel and the card that replaces it are the same
          colour and the swap is not a change of place. */}
      <div className="overflow-hidden rounded-[28px] border border-[var(--rc-hairline)] p-8 text-center shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)] md:p-12">
        <p className="rc__label">Section finished</p>
@@NEW
    {/* `.rc` is what paints `--rc-page`, so the rounding and the border belong
        on the same element: a square page-coloured box behind a rounded border
        shows its corners over the violet ground. */}
    <div className="rc mx-auto max-w-2xl overflow-hidden rounded-[28px] border border-[var(--rc-hairline)] shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)]">
      <div className="p-8 text-center md:p-12">
        <p className="rc__label">Section finished</p>
@@END
PATCH
node "$SP/patch.mjs" "$SP/s7o.patch"
[/external_agent_tool_call]

Step 7 of the 2026-09-09 drill audit. Measured on both harnesses before and
after.

The clock. The Report Card read the stored `within_time` flag and the launch
surface preferred it, so three surfaces answered the same question from three
places and none of them checked the answer against the figure printed beside
it. `recall/sectionClock.ts` derives it once, from the two numbers she can
see. The grader writes the flag from exactly that comparison, so nothing
changes for a run graded since the flag existed; what changes is everything
else. A row written before the flag was `undefined`, matched neither branch on
the card, and got a time with no verdict at all. A row whose flag disagrees
with its own clock now loses to the clock. In the harness, 28:32 against a
twenty-five minute goal said "Inside the time" and now says "Over the goal";
24:00 said "Over the goal" and now says nothing, because she made it.

A test was wrong in the same way. `runStats.test.ts` asserted that 1450 and
1665 seconds are both inside 25:00. 1665 is 27:45. The fixture carried
`within_time: true` to match the mistaken arithmetic and the code trusted the
flag, so the error was invisible from both ends. The fixture is corrected to
1465, which is inside the clock AND outside `within_goal`, so the assertion
now proves what its name claims.

One name for a number. "Recalled 73/75" on the door counted stimuli she spoke
into, not how much came back: a student could record every stimulus in a
section and recall almost nothing. It is Recorded, in the stat strip, in the
run table, and in `SectionStats`. The door's run table gains Held, the card's
own section figure under the card's own name, so the number she remembers
from one surface exists on the other. Per stimulus, the figure now sits beside
the words: "95% held. Almost all of it came back." Joshua's call, 2026-09-09:
percentages are allowed per stimulus and at section level. The older ruling
stands and is untouched, because it bans Detail arithmetic, not the
percentage.

One ground. The handover warmed to the Activity Centre's cream, rgb(244,239,231),
and then the marking wait drew a cream Activity Centre panel on the violet
ground the report phase paints, and then the card arrived. Three grounds in
ten seconds, at the end of twenty-five unbroken minutes. The field now warms
to what `.rc-ground` actually paints, and the wait is drawn in `.rc`, so it is
the card's own surface and the card replacing it is a change of content rather
than of place. Contrast improves either way: the handover inks measure 14.18:1
and 8.28:1 on the new ground, the wait's 15.8:1, 6.34:1 and 5.63:1 on the
card's page.

Note the audit said to warm to `--rc-page`. That is the card, not the page
behind it; `.rc-ground` is one step darker so the card still reads as a card,
and warming to `--rc-page` would have left the same visible step one shade
smaller. A test now holds the copied value to the one it copies.
